\left(\tilde{L}_{1}\cdots\tilde{L}_{k}w\right)_{|\partial M}=0\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})

and this gives (
53
).

Now, we prove (
51
) in the case
F\neq 0
. Note that the previous proof gives

\left\|\partial_{\nu}u\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s}}+\left\|F\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})},

a weaker result. By linearity, we may assume that
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)=0
. By density, it suffices to prove that for
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}([0,T],\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\inte M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
, one has

\left\|\partial_{\nu}\Psi\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|F\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T),H^{s})}

where
\Psi
is the Duhamel term defined above. Fix
k\in\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket
, and let
L_{1},\cdots,L_{k}
be smooth vector fields on the Riemannian manifold
\partial M
. We prove

\left\|L_{1}\cdots L_{k}\partial_{t}^{s-k}\partial_{\nu}\Psi\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|F\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T),H^{s})}.

For
\tau\in[0,T]
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, one has

\partial_{t}^{2k}u_{\tau}(0)=0,\quad 2k\in\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket,

and

\partial_{t}^{2k+1}u_{\tau}(0)=\mathsf{P}^{k}F(\tau),\quad 2k+1\in\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket.

Hence, for
k\in\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket
, there exists a differential operator
R_{k}
such that

\partial_{t}^{k}\Psi(t)=(R_{k}F)(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}^{k}u_{\tau}(t-\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.

As
F(t)
is compactly supported in
\inte M
, this gives

\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{\nu}\Psi(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{\nu}u_{\tau}(t-\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Hence, one has

\displaystyle\left\|L_{1}\cdots L_{k}\partial_{t}^{s-k}\partial_{\nu}\Psi\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{\tau\leq t}L_{1}\cdots L_{k}\partial_{t}^{s-k}\partial_{\nu}u_{\tau}(t-\tau,x)\mathrm{d}\tau\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left\|L_{1}\cdots L_{k}\partial_{t}^{s-k}\partial_{\nu}u_{\tau}\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\partial_{\nu}u_{\tau}\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\mathrm{d}\tau.

By (
51
) in the case
F=0
, we get

\left\|L_{1}\cdots L_{k}\partial_{t}^{s-k}\partial_{\nu}\Psi\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\left\|F(\tau)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{s}}\mathrm{d}\tau=\|F\|_{L^{1}((0,T),H^{s})}.

The case
s<0
.
Note that in the sense of classical trace theorem, the normal derivative of a solution does not exist in that case. Take
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
s<0
, and
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s}
. There exist
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
and
\alpha\in\{1,2\}
such that
s=-2\sigma+\alpha
. Let
\left(\tilde{u}^{0},\tilde{u}^{1}\right)
be the unique element of
\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{\alpha}
such that

\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)=\left(\mathcal{S}_{\!s+1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\tilde{u}^{0},\mathcal{S}_{\!s+\sigma}^{\sigma}\tilde{u}^{1}\right).

Recall that the solution
u
associated with
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)
is defined by
u=\mathcal{S}_{\!s+1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\tilde{u}
, where
\tilde{u}
is the solution associated with
\left(\tilde{u}^{0},\tilde{u}^{1}\right)
. Using Proposition
12
, we can write

\displaystyle u(t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{\!s+2}^{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{S}_{\!\alpha}^{1}\left(\tilde{u}(t)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{P}_{\!\!s+2}+i\mu\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+3}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}\right)\circ\cdots\circ\left(\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\alpha}+i\mu\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\alpha-1}}\right)\left(\tilde{u}(t)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1-2k}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\alpha+1-k}^{k}\tilde{u}(t)\right)

and by Theorem
16
-
(i)
, we get

u(t)=\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1-2k}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\tilde{u}(t)\right)

for
t\in\mathbb{R}
. As
\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}\in H^{\alpha}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, we define
\partial_{\nu}u
by

\partial_{\nu}u=\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{H^{\alpha-2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}\right)

where
\iota_{H^{\alpha-2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}
is the embedding from
H^{\alpha-2k}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
to
H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Clearly, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\nu}u\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\left\|\iota_{H^{\alpha-2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}\right)\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}\right\|_{H^{\alpha}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\left(\tilde{u}^{0},\tilde{u}^{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\left\|\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s}}.

To complete the proof, one has to show the two additional results of Theorem
16
-
(iii)
.

Connection with the usual normal derivative.
Here, we show that our definition of the normal derivative of a solution coincide with the usual normal derivative for a regular solution. More precisely, we prove that for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\delta>0
and
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta}
, one has

\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u.

(54)

By interpolation, it suffices to prove (
54
) for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Lemma 40
.

For
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
s\leq-1
, and
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{2}\times\mathcal{K}^{1}
, one has

\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u.

Proof.

Write
s=-2\sigma+\alpha
, with
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
and
\alpha\in\{1,2\}
, and let
\left(\tilde{u}^{0},\tilde{u}^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{\alpha}
be given by

\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u^{0},\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s}}u^{1}\right)=\left(\mathcal{S}_{\!s+1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\tilde{u}^{0},\mathcal{S}_{\!s+\sigma}^{\sigma}\tilde{u}^{1}\right).

One has
\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u=\mathcal{S}_{\!s+1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\tilde{u}
, where
\tilde{u}
is the solution with initial data
\left(\tilde{u}^{0},\tilde{u}^{1}\right)
. By definition, one has

\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{H^{\alpha-2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}\right).

(55)

Writing

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{u}^{0},\tilde{u}^{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\mathcal{S}_{\!s+1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}\left(u^{0}\right),\left(\mathcal{S}_{\!s+\sigma}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s}}\left(u^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2\sigma+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1}}\circ\left(\mathcal{S}_{2+\sigma}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\left(u^{0}\right),\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2\sigma+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\alpha}}\circ\left(\mathcal{S}_{1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\left(u^{1}\right)\right),

one finds
\tilde{u}=\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2\sigma+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\alpha+1}}v
, where
v
is the solution with initial data

\left(\left(\mathcal{S}_{2+\sigma}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}u^{0},\left(\mathcal{S}_{1+\sigma}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{2\sigma+2}\times\mathcal{K}^{2\sigma+1}.

One also has
u=\mathcal{S}_{2+\sigma}^{\sigma}v
. As
\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}=\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}v
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, one finds

\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}=\iota_{H^{2\sigma+1-2k}\rightarrow H^{\alpha-2k}}\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}v

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Coming back to (
55
), one obtains

\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{H^{1+2\sigma-2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}v\right).

For
k\in\llbracket 0,\sigma\rrbracket
, one has
1+2\sigma-2k\geq 1
implying

\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{H^{1+2\sigma-2k}\rightarrow H^{1}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}v\right).

(56)

Omitting the embeddings in
H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, one finds

\sum_{k=0}^{\sigma}\binom{\sigma}{k}(i\mu)^{\sigma-k}\iota_{H^{1+2\sigma-2k}\rightarrow H^{1}}\left(\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}v\right)=\partial_{\nu}\left(\mathcal{S}_{2+\sigma}^{\sigma}v\right)=\partial_{\nu}u.

Together with (
56
), this completes the proof.
∎

To prove (
54
), we distinguish three cases. First, if
s+1\geq 0
then (
54
) is true. Second, if
s+1\leq-1
and
s+\delta+1\geq 2
, then using Lemma
40
and the first case, one finds

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{2}}u\right)

\displaystyle=\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{2}}u\right)

\displaystyle=\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\circ\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{1}}\left(\partial_{\nu}u\right)

\displaystyle=\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u.

Finally, if
s+1\leq-1
and
s+\delta+1<2
, then we consider an approximation of
u
: take a sequence
\left(\left(u_{k}^{0},u_{k}^{1}\right)\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of elements of
\mathcal{K}^{2}\times\mathcal{K}^{1}
such that

\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}}u_{k}^{0},\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta}}u_{k}^{1}\right)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right).

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
u_{k}
be the solution associated with
\left(u_{k}^{0},u_{k}^{1}\right)
. Set

w_{k}=\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u-\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u_{k}.

As
w_{k}
is a solution of the wave equation, one has

\left\|\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)-\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u_{k}\right)\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\left(w_{k}(0),\partial_{t}w_{k}(0)\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s}}.

Writing

\left(w_{k}(0),\partial_{t}w_{k}(0)\right)=\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}\left(u^{0}-\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}}u_{k}^{0}\right),\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s}}\left(u^{1}-\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta}}u_{k}^{1}\right)\right)

one finds

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)-\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u_{k}\right)\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\left(u^{0}-\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}}u_{k}^{0},u^{1}-\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta}}u_{k}^{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta}}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

(57)

On the other hand, using Lemma
40
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u-\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u_{k}\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{s}}\left(\partial_{\nu}u-\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s+\delta}}\partial_{\nu}u_{k}\right)\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{s}}\left(\partial_{\nu}u-\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}}u_{k}\right)\right)\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\nu}u-\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+\delta+1}}u_{k}\right)\right\|_{H^{s+\delta}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

As above, one finds

\left\|\iota_{H^{s+\delta}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u-\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u_{k}\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

(58)

Lemma
40
gives
\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u_{k}=\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u_{k}\right)
, and with (
57
) and (
58
), this completes the proof of the third case.

The normal derivative and the time-derivative commute.
Take
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s}
. Here, we show that

\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2k}u=\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}u.

(59)

Note that the left-hand side is well-defined as we know that
\partial_{t}^{2k}u
is a solution of the wave equation. By interpolation, we may assume that
s\in\mathbb{Z}
. If
s-2k\geq 0
, then (
59
) holds true, so we can assume that
s-2k\leq-1
. As above, using an approximation, it suffices to prove that

\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2k}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2k+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2k+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)

(60)

for all
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{2k+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{2k}
. Using (
54
), one finds

\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2k}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2k+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s-2k+1}}\partial_{t}^{2k}u\right)=\iota_{L^{2}\rightarrow H^{s-2k}}\left(\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2k}u\right).

Note that
\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2k}u=\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}u
, as
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{2k+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{2k}
. One has
\partial_{\nu}u=\iota_{H^{2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, implying

\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}u=\partial_{t}^{2k}\iota_{H^{2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u

in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. This gives

\iota_{L^{2}\rightarrow H^{s-2k}}\partial_{t}^{2k}\partial_{\nu}u=\partial_{t}^{2k}\iota_{H^{2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u.

Hence, one obtains

\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2k}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2k+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{s+1}}u\right)=\partial_{t}^{2k}\iota_{H^{2k}\rightarrow H^{s}}\partial_{\nu}u.

Using (
54
) again, one finds (
60
).

B.2.2
Proof of Theorem
18
in negative regularity

Here, we prove Theorem
18
for
s\leq 0
. An integration by parts gives the following identity.

Lemma 41
.

For
u
and
v
in

\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))

one has

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}\right)u,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle u,\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\left\langle\partial_{t}u(t),v(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle u(t),\partial_{t}v(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\right]_{0}^{T}-\left\langle\left\langle X,\nu\right\rangle_{g}u,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\ -\left\langle\partial_{\nu}u,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\langle u,\partial_{\nu}v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Step 1: Definition of the solution.

Take
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. If there exists a smooth solution
v
of (
11
), then for all smooth function
u
, one has

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}\right)u,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle\partial_{t}u(T),v(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle u(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\left\langle X,\nu\right\rangle_{g}u+\partial_{\nu}u,\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\langle u,\partial_{\nu}v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

In particular, if
u
is such that
u_{|(0,T)\times\partial M}=0
and
\left(u(T),\partial_{t}u(T)\right)=0
then

\left\langle\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}\right)u,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=-\left\langle\partial_{\nu}u,\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Hence, if
u
is a smooth solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}u-\mathsf{P}u&=&F&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
\left(u(T,\cdot),\partial_{t}u(T,\cdot)\right)&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
u&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M.\end{array}\right.

(61)

then one has

\left\langle F,v\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=-\left\langle\partial_{\nu}u,\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

We use this as the definition of
v
. More precisely, take
s\leq 0
and define

\begin{array}[]{cccc}L_{s}:&L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))&\longrightarrow&H_{0}^{-s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\\
&F&\longmapsto&-\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}u\end{array}

where
u
is the solution of (
61
). By Theorem
16
, the operator
L_{s}
is well-defined and continuous. For
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, we define the solution
v
of (
11
) by
v=L_{s}^{\ast}f
. One has
v\in L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
. In the next step, we show that
v
is more regular.

Step 2: Regularity of the solution.

Fix
s\leq 0
. In this step, we show that for
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, one has

v=L_{s}^{\ast}(f)\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{s-2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))

(62)

with an inequality, and
\partial_{t}^{2}v=\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. To get (
62
) for all
f
, it suffices to show that (
62
) holds for
f
smooth, with an inequality of the form

\left\|v\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{0}(H^{s})\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}(H^{s-1})\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}(H^{s-2})}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

(63)

Proof of (
62
).

Suppose
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and denote by
\tilde{f}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
an extension of
\diag(\Theta)f
. One writes
v=\tilde{f}+w
, where
w
is a solution of the wave equation with homogeneous Dirichlet boundary condition, as follows. Set

F=-\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)\tilde{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N}).

Since
F\in L^{1}((0,T),L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}((0,T),H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
, the solution
w
of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}w-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}w&=&F&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
\left(w(0,\cdot),\partial_{t}w(0,\cdot)\right)&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
w&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M,\end{array}\right.

(64)

is well-defined, and
w\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
by Theorem
16
-
(iv)
. We claim that
v=\tilde{f}+w
, that is,

\left\langle v,\phi\right\rangle_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}),L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s})}=\left\langle w+\tilde{f},\overline{\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

(65)

for all
\phi\in L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s}(M))
. By density, it suffices to prove (
65
) for
\phi\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\inte M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Note that Lemma
41
does not apply to
w+\tilde{f}
, due to the lack of regularity of
w
. However, it applies to
\tilde{f}
, and Lemma
39
can be used for
w
. Consider
\phi\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\inte M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and let
u
be the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}u-\mathsf{P}u&=&\phi&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
\left(u(T,\cdot),\partial_{t}u(T,\cdot)\right)&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
u&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M.\end{array}\right.

(66)

One has

\displaystyle\left\langle w+\tilde{f},\overline{\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle w,\overline{\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\langle\tilde{f},\overline{\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle F,\overline{u}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\langle\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)\tilde{f},\overline{u}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle\tilde{f},\partial_{\nu}\overline{u}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\langle\diag(\Theta)f,\partial_{\nu}\overline{u}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})},

where one has used the fact that
\tilde{f}
is compactly supported in
(0,T)
. This implies

\left\langle w+\tilde{f},\overline{\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle f,\overline{L_{s}\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle v,\phi\right\rangle_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}),L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s})},

as
v=L_{s}^{\ast}f
. This proves (
65
). In particular, this gives (
62
). Note that, for now, (
62
) has only been proved for smooth
f
.

Proof of (
63
).

We prove (
63
) for
f
smooth. First, note that the operator

L_{s}^{\ast}:H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\longrightarrow L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))

is continuous, as
L_{s}
is continuous. This gives

\left\|v\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})},

implying
v=L_{s}^{\ast}f\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
if
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
.

Second, we prove

\left\|\partial_{t}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

(67)

Consider
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and
\phi\in\mathscr{C}\mathscr{c}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\inte M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. By definition, one has

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}v,\phi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),\mathscr{D}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle v,\partial_{t}\phi\right\rangle_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}),L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\diag(\Theta)f,\overline{\partial_{\nu}\tilde{u}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

where
\tilde{u}
is the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{u}-\mathsf{P}\tilde{u}&=&\partial_{t}\phi&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
(\tilde{u}(T,\cdot),\partial_{t}\tilde{u}(T,\cdot))&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
\tilde{u}&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M.\end{array}\right.

Note that
\tilde{u}=\partial_{t}u
, where
u
is the solution of (
66
). Indeed, one has
\partial_{t}u(T)=0
and

\partial_{t}^{2}u(T)=\mathsf{P}u(T)+\phi(T)=0

as
\phi
is compactly supported. Hence, one finds

\left|\left\langle\partial_{t}v,\phi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),\mathscr{D}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\right|\leq\left\|\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}u\right\|_{H^{-s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

By Theorem
16
, one has

\left\|\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}u\right\|_{H^{-s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\partial_{\nu}u\right\|_{H^{-s+1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))}.

As
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times M,\inte\mathbb{C}^{N})
is dense in
L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
, this gives (
67
).

Third, the proof of

\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s-2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

is similar: one writes

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\partial_{t}^{2}v,\phi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),\mathscr{D}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2}u\right\|_{H^{-s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{-s+2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Note that the equality
\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{2}u=\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{\nu}u
is obvious, as
\phi
is smooth.

Connection with the wave equation.

We show that for
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, one has

\partial_{t}^{2}v-\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v=0,\quad\text{ in }\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N}).

(68)

As
v=L_{s}^{\ast}f\in L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
is continuous with respect to
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, we may assume that
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. As above, write
v=w+\tilde{f}
, where
\tilde{f}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
is an extension of
\diag(\Theta)f
, and
w
is the solution of (
64
). For all
t\in[0,T]
, one has
\partial_{t}^{2}w(t)-\mathsf{P}_{\!0}^{\ast}w(t)=-\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)\tilde{f}(t)
in
H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Hence, one obtains

\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}\right)v(t)=-\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)\tilde{f}(t)+\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)\tilde{f}(t)=0,\quad t\in[0,T],

in
H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. This gives (
68
).

Step 3: The additional regularity result.

Here, we complete the proof of Theorem
18
for
s\leq 0
, by proving

\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s-1},

and the duality equality (
12
), for
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
s\leq 0
. We start with some remarks. We know that
v(T)\in H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, that is,
v(T)
is a continuous linear form on
H_{0}^{-s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. As one has
H_{0}^{-s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\subset\mathcal{K}^{-s}
, we prove that
v(T)
can be extended as a continuous linear form on
\mathcal{K}^{-s}
. Such an extension is not unique: however, we seek an extension such that (
12
) holds true, and

\left\|v(T)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

(69)

The same remarks can be made for
\partial_{t}v(T)
.

Consider
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and write
v
,
\tilde{f}
,
F
and
w
as above. Because of the support of
\tilde{f}
, one has
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)=\left(w(T),\partial_{t}w(T)\right)
in
H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\times H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Consider
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{2}\times\mathcal{K}^{1}
, and write
u
for the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}u-\mathsf{P}u&=&0&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
\left(u(T,\cdot),\partial_{t}u(T,\cdot)\right)&=&\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)&&\text{in }M,\\
u&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M.\end{array}\right.

(70)

Applying Lemma
41
to
u
and
\tilde{f}
, one finds

\left\langle u,\left(\partial_{t}^{2}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\right)\tilde{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle\partial_{\nu}u,\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

By Lemma
39
, one has

\left\langle u,F\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle u^{0},\partial_{t}w(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle u^{1},w(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

Thus, one obtains

\left\langle u^{1},v(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle u^{0},\partial_{t}v(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle\partial_{\nu}u,\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

If
s\leq-1
, then this is in particular true for all
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in H_{0}^{-s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\times H_{0}^{-s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. If
s=0
, then this is true for all
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in H_{0}^{-s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\times H_{0}^{-s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
by density. Hence, one has

\left\langle v(T),u^{1}\right\rangle_{H^{s},H_{0}^{-s}}-\left\langle\partial_{t}v(T),u^{0}\right\rangle_{H^{s-1},H_{0}^{-s+1}}=\left\langle f,\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}u\right\rangle_{H^{s},H_{0}^{-s}}

(71)

for all
f
smooth. By density and continuity, this is true for all
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Yet, the right-hand side is well-defined for
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{-s}
, and one has

\left|\left\langle f,\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}u\right\rangle_{H^{s},H_{0}^{-s}}\right|\lesssim\left\|\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{s}}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Hence, (
71
) yields a unique extension of
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)
as a linear form on
\mathcal{K}^{-s}\times\mathcal{K}^{1-s}
, which satisfies (
12
) and (
69
).

B.2.3
Proof of Theorem
18
in positive regularity

Here, we prove Theorem
18
for
s>0
. We know how to construct the solution
v
of (
11
) if
f\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and one has

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})).

If
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
with
s>0
, one can define
v
as in the case
s=0
. We show that

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{s-2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})).

We will need the following regularity result, which is an easy consequence of the corresponding scalar result. Set
W=\left\{u\in L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\right\}
.

Lemma 42
.

The Dirichlet trace
H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\rightarrow H^{\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
has a continuous extension as an operator from
W
to
H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and there exists
C>0
such that

\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\left(\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\right),\quad u\in W.

In addition, for
m\in\mathbb{N}
, if
u\in L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
satisfies
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\in H^{m-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
u_{|\partial M}\in H^{m+\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, then
u\in H^{m+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and

\|u\|_{H^{m+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\leq C\left(\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{m-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{m+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\right)

with
C>0
independent of
u
.

Proof.

This result is well-know in the scalar case
N=1
. We show that the vector-valued case is a consequence of the scalar case.

Take
u=(u^{1},\cdots,u^{N})\in L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
such that
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Write
(\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{N})
for the projections associated with the canonical basis of
\mathbb{C}^{N}
. For
k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
, one has
u^{k}\in L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C})
and

\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u^{k}-\pi^{k}\left(Xu+qu\right)\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C})

so that
\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u^{k}\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C})
. Hence, the scalar case gives
u_{|\partial M}\in H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, with

\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Writing

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|Xu+qu\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})},

one obtains the first part of the lemma.

We prove the second part of the lemma by induction. Start with
m=0
and
u\in L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
such that
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
u_{|\partial M}\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. As above, for
k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
, one has
\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u^{k}\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C})
, so the scalar case gives
u\in H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and

\displaystyle\|u\|_{H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|Xu+qu\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Finally, assume that the result holds for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Take
u\in L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
such that
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\in H^{(m+1)-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
u_{|\partial M}\in H^{m+1+\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. By induction,
u\in H^{m+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and

\|u\|_{H^{m+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{m-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{m+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

(72)

Hence, for
k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
, one has

\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u^{k}=\pi^{k}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u+Xu+qu\right)\in H^{m}(M,\mathbb{C})

so the scalar case gives
u^{k}\in H^{m+2}(M,\mathbb{C})
and

\displaystyle\|u\|_{H^{m+2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\Delta_{\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{m}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{m+\frac{3}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{m}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|Xu+qu\right\|_{H^{m}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{m+\frac{3}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{m}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{H^{m+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{m+\frac{3}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Using (
72
), one finds

\|u\|_{H^{m+2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\lesssim\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}u\right\|_{H^{m}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{m+\frac{3}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

and this completes the proof.
∎

We prove Theorem
18
by induction on
s\geq 1
.

Step 1: The case
s=1
.

Fix
f\in H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and write
v
for the associated solution. From the case
s=0
, one has

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{-2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))

and
\partial_{t}^{2}v=\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. We prove first that

v\in\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))

(73)

with an inequality. Then, using Lemma
42
, we show that

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})),

(74)

with an inequality, and

v(t)_{|\partial M}=\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)_{|\{t\}\times\partial M}

(75)

in
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

We prove (
73
). Let
\tilde{v}
be the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{v}-\mathsf{P}^{\ast}\tilde{v}&=&0&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
(\tilde{v}(0,\cdot),\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(0,\cdot))&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
\tilde{v}&=&\partial_{t}(\diag(\Theta)f)&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M.\end{array}\right.

(76)

For
\tilde{\Theta}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
such that for all
k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
,
\pi_{k}\tilde{\Theta}=1
in a neighbourhood of
\supp\pi_{k}\Theta
, one has
\partial_{t}\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)=\diag(\tilde{\Theta})\partial_{t}\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)
, implying that
\tilde{v}
is well-defined. One has

\partial_{t}\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),

yielding
\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
by Theorem
18
in the case
s=0
. We show that
\partial_{t}v=\tilde{v}
. Consider
\phi\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\inte M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Let
u
and
\tilde{u}
be the solutions of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}u-\mathsf{P}u&=&\phi&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
\left(u(T,\cdot),\partial_{t}u(T,\cdot)\right)&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
u&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M,\end{array}\right.

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{u}-\mathsf{P}\tilde{u}&=&\partial_{t}\phi&&\text{in }(0,T)\times M,\\
(\tilde{u}(T,\cdot),\partial_{t}\tilde{u}(T,\cdot))&=&0&&\text{in }M,\\
\tilde{u}&=&0&&\text{on }(0,T)\times\partial M.\end{array}\right.

One has
\partial_{t}u=\tilde{u}
, yielding, by definition of
v
and
\tilde{v}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial_{t}v,\phi\right\rangle_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{-1}),L^{1}((0,T),H_{0}^{1})}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle v,\partial_{t}\overline{\phi}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle f,\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}\overline{\tilde{u}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial_{t}\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right),\partial_{\nu}\overline{u}\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\tilde{v},\phi\right\rangle_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}),L^{1}((0,T),L^{2})}.

This gives (
73
). In addition, one has

\displaystyle\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],L^{2})\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|v\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],L^{2})\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{-1})}+\left\|\tilde{v}\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],L^{2})\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Now, we prove (
74
). Consider
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and write
v=w+\tilde{f}
as in the proof of Theorem
18
in negative regularity. As
w\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
, (
74
) is true for
f
smooth. To get (
74
) for all
f
, we prove

\left\|v\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T),H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

(77)

For
t\in[0,T]
, one has

\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v(t)=\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\in H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})

and

v(t)_{|\partial M}=w(t)_{|\partial M}+\tilde{f}(t)_{|\partial M}=0+\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)_{|\{t\}\times\partial M}

(78)

in
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Hence, Lemma
42
gives

\displaystyle\|v(t)\|_{H^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\right\|_{H^{-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)_{|\{t\}\times\partial M}\right\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

for all
t\in[0,T]
. This gives (
77
). By density, it holds for all
f\in H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, yielding (
74
). Note also that (
75
) holds for smooth
f
by (
78
). As both sides of (
75
) are continuous with respect to
f\in H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, we obtain (
75
) for all
f\in H^{1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
.

Step 2: The case
s\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
.

We show by induction on
s\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
that

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{s-2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})),

(79)

if
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, with an inequality, and
v(t)_{|\partial M}=\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)_{|\{t\}\times\partial M}
in
H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
for
t\in[0,T]
. Assume that the result holds for some
s\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
, and consider
f\in H^{s+1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
.

By induction, one has
v(t)_{|\partial M}=\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)_{|\{t\}\times\partial M}
in
H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
for
t\in[0,T]
. In particular, this gives
v(t)_{|\partial M}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. As in the case
s=1
, one has
\partial_{t}v=\tilde{v}
, where
\tilde{v}
is the solution of (
76
), and this gives

\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{s})\cap\mathscr{C}^{2}([0,T],H^{s-1})}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{s+1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))},

since
\tilde{v}
fulfills (
79
). In particular, one has
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v(t)=\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\in H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
for
t\in[0,T]
. Hence, for
t\in[0,T]
, Lemma
42
gives
v(t)\in H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and

\displaystyle\left\|v(t)\right\|_{H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v(t)\right\|_{H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|v(t)_{|\partial M}\right\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|v(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\right\|_{H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|\left(\diag(\Theta)f\right)_{|\{t\}\times\partial M}\right\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s+1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

This gives
v\in L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
. To complete the proof, it suffices to show that

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))

for
f
smooth. For such
f
, Lemma
42
gives

\displaystyle\left\|v(t+\varepsilon)-v(t)\right\|_{H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(t+\varepsilon)-\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\right\|_{H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|f(t+\varepsilon)-f(t)\right\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}+\left\|v(t+\varepsilon)-v(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})},

and one concludes

\left\|v(t+\varepsilon)-v(t)\right\|_{H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}\underset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}0.

This gives (
79
).

Step 3: The additional regularity result.

Here, we complete the proof of Theorem
18
by proving

\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s-1}

(80)

for
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
and
s\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
. We will also prove the duality equality of Theorem
18
. As
\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s-1}
is a closed subspace of
H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\times H^{s-1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, it suffices to prove (
80
) for
f
smooth. We proceed by induction. The result is true for
s=0
, but one has to treat the case
s=1
separately.

Case 1:

s=1
. Take
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and write
v
for the associated solution. We prove that
v(T)\in H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. Write
v=w+\tilde{f}
as above. One has
w\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
, implying

v(T)=w(T)+\tilde{f}(T)=w(T)\in H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),

since
\tilde{f}
is compactly supported in
(0,T)\times M
. Note that
w\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s})
for
s\geq 2
is not always true, preventing a straightforward generalization of this argument.

Case 2:

s
odd. Assume that the result holds true for some odd
s\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
. Write
s=2\sigma+1
, and consider
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. We prove that
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+2}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}
. By induction, one has
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma}
, and as
f\in H^{s+1}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, we know that

v\in\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})).

By definition,
H^{2\sigma+2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}=\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+2}
, implying
v(T)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+2}
. Set
\tilde{v}=\partial_{t}v
, solution to (
76
). By induction, one has
\partial_{t}v(T)=\tilde{v}(T)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}
, completing the proof in the case
s
is odd.

Case 3:

s
even. Assume that the result holds for some even
s\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
. Write
s=2\sigma
, and consider
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
. We show that
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma}
. By induction, one has
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma-1}
, and we know that
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in H^{s+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})\times H^{s}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})
, yielding
\partial_{t}v(T)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma}
as above. The definition of
\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}
reads

\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}=\left\{u\in H^{2\sigma+1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\cap\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma},\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast\ \sigma}u\in H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))\right\},

implying that one has
v(T)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma+1}
, if
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast\ \sigma}v(T)\in H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}))
. Again, write
\tilde{v}=\partial_{t}v
. One has
\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast}v(T)=\partial_{t}^{2}v(T)=\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(T)
, and by induction, one finds
\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(T)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{2\sigma-1}
. Thus, as
\sigma>0
, one obtains

\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast\ \sigma}v(T)=\mathsf{P}_{\!\!\mathscr{D}^{\prime}}^{\ast\ \sigma-1}\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(T)\in H_{0}^{1}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N}),

completing the proof in the case
s
is even.

Finally, we prove the duality equality of Theorem
18
for
s\geq 1
. Consider
s\geq 1
,
f\in H^{s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C^{N}})
and
\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\in\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{-s}
. Write
u
for the solution of (
70
). We show that

\left\langle u^{1},v(T)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{K}^{-s+1},\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s-1}}-\left\langle u^{0},\partial_{t}v(T)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{K}^{-s},\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}}=\left\langle\partial_{\nu}u,\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{H^{-s},H_{0}^{s}}.

(81)

With the approximation result of Theorem
16
, consider a sequence
\left((u_{k}^{0},u_{k}^{1})\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of elements of
\mathcal{K}^{2}\times\mathcal{K}^{1}
such that

\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}}u_{k}^{0},\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s}}u_{k}^{1}\right)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\left(u^{0},u^{1}\right)\text{ in }\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}\times\mathcal{K}^{-s}.

If
u_{k}
is the solution of (
70
) associated with
\left(u_{k}^{0},u_{k}^{1}\right)
, then one has

\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}}u_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}u\text{ in }\mathscr{C}^{0}([0,T],\mathcal{K}^{-s+1})\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}([0,T],\mathcal{K}^{-s}),

and

\partial_{\nu}\left(\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}}u_{k}\right)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\partial_{\nu}u\text{ in }H^{-s}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N}).

The duality equality of Theorem
18
for
s=0
gives

\left\langle u_{k}^{1},v(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}-\left\langle u_{k}^{0},\partial_{t}v(T)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}=\left\langle\diag(\Theta)\partial_{\nu}u_{k},f\right\rangle_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\partial M,\mathbb{C}^{N})}.

Since
\left(v(T),\partial_{t}v(T)\right)\in\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}\times\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s-1}
, one finds

\left\langle\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}}u_{k}^{1},v(T)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{K}^{-s+1},\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s-1}}-\left\langle\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s}}u_{k}^{0},\partial_{t}v(T)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{K}^{-s},\mathcal{K}_{\ast}^{s}}=\left\langle\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{-s}}\partial_{\nu}u_{k},\diag(\Theta)f\right\rangle_{H^{-s},H_{0}^{s}}.

From Theorem
16
, one has
\iota_{H^{1}\rightarrow H^{-s}}\partial_{\nu}u_{k}=\partial_{\nu}\iota_{\mathcal{K}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{-s+1}}u_{k}
, yielding (
81
).
